### [v0.28.0](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases/tag/v0.28.0)

**来源**: vLLM GitHub Releases

**摘要**: <h1>v0.28.0</h1>
<h2>Highlights</h2>
<p>This release features 584 commits from 270 contributors (76 new)!</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Kimi-K3 performance push</strong>: a major optimization effort for Kimi-K3 across the stack — Decode Context Parallel (DCP) support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50484">#50484</a>), fused FlashKDA decode and prefill kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50654">#50654</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51311">#51311</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52458">#52458</a>), SiTU activation support for MegaMoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50510">#50510</a>), GEMM-RS for sequence parallelism (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52079">#52079</a>), combined all-gathers with 1.5~3x kernel-level speedup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51070">#51070</a>), an adaptive speculative token budget delivering ~60% better DSpark TTFT (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51725">#51725</a>), and optional shared-expert sharding saving ~17 GiB of memory per GPU (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50912">#50912</a>). Kimi-K3 also now runs on ROCm with the V2 model runner (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51653">#51653</a>).</li>
<li><strong>DeepSeek V4</strong>: sparse MLA now works end-to-end for plain decode, MTP, and DSpark speculative decoding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51538">#51538</a>), joined by AMD Quark NVFP4 support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47972">#47972</a>), reasoning-effort prompts and mappings (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50580">#50580</a>), sparse top-k metadata kernel optimizations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52084">#52084</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51967">#51967</a>), narrowed eager CUDA graph regions (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51430">#51430</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52401">#52401</a>), and ROCm enablement on gfx11 and gfx950 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47017">#47017</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52212">#52212</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding advances</strong>: DFlash2 with local convolution and a candidate selector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52816">#52816</a>), DSpark confidence-scheduled verification (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47808">#47808</a>), and async scheduling auto-enabled for draft models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48341">#48341</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Model Runner V2 maturation</strong>: E/P/D disaggregation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/38390">#38390</a>), weight offloading (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51413">#51413</a>), multi-layer MTP KV cache support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50062">#50062</a>), encoder CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49852">#49852</a>), decoder token-wise pooling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50931">#50931</a>) plus Transformers pooling models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52425">#52425</a>), attention-free models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52374">#52374</a>), and <code>thinking_token_budget</code> support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46727">#46727</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Tiered KV cache offloading</strong>: disk offloading support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49644">#49644</a>), out-of-tree secondary tier managers via <code>module_path</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51007">#51007</a>), partial secondary-tier load results (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50321">#50321</a>), tiering metrics (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48798">#48798</a>), and a canonical CPU layout for parallelism-agnostic offload (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48414">#48414</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Rust frontend &amp; gRPC</strong>: a standalone renderer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50289">#50289</a>), multimodal image inference over gRPC (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50368">#50368</a>), explicit data-parallel rank routing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51178">#51178</a>), and RL lifecycle control (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51316">#51316</a>), with protobuf schemas now published to Buf (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51276">#51276</a>).</li>
<li><strong>New defaults</strong>: <code>max_num_batched_tokens</code> raised from 8192 to 16384 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51726">#51726</a>), prefix caching enabled by default for Mamba models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50991">#50991</a>), and the Blackwell CUDA graph capture default raised to 1024 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49390">#49390</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Breaking changes</strong>: bitsandbytes support migrated to an out-of-tree plugin (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43529">#43529</a>); Transformers bumped to 5.15.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51668">#51668</a>); the deprecated <code>calculate_kv_scales</code> runtime KV scale calculation was removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49389">#49389</a>); <code>override_attention_dtype</code> was removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48684">#48684</a>).</li>
</ul>

<h3>Other Artifacts</h3>
<p>Pre-built release artifacts are available in the <strong>Assets</strong> section at the bottom of this page, including:</p>
<ul>
<li>Source distribution tarball</li>
<li>CUDA 12.9 Python wheels for x86_64 and arm64</li>
<li>CUDA 13.0 Python wheels for x86_64 and arm64</li>
<li>CPU Python wheels for x86_64, arm64, and macOS</li>

<h2>Model Support</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: Muse Glimmer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51655">#51655</a>), Ling 3.0 Flash with BF16, MTP, and parser support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51045">#51045</a>) plus an FP8 variant (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51265">#51265</a>) and hybrid MXFP4 routed experts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52114">#52114</a>), Dots3 NOTE native multimodal support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51255">#51255</a>), and Interns2mobius (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51149">#51149</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Qwen</strong>: Qwen3.8 enabled on AMD ROCm (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50068">#50068</a>), fused CUDA post-conv MTP decode kernel for Qwen3.5 GDN (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51674">#51674</a>), GDN gates aligned with speculative tokens (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51812">#51812</a>), and Qwen3.5 fixes for text-only checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50734">#50734</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50355">#50355</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Transformers modeling backend</strong>: MLA support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48250">#48250</a>), hardware-agnostic model definition (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49458">#49458</a>), fully generalized input embedding handling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51247">#51247</a>), logit softcapping (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52173">#52173</a>), and a hardened multimodal path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51408">#51408</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51657">#51657</a>).</li>
<li><strong>LoRA</strong>: vision tower LoRA for Gemma4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/42662">#42662</a>), tower/connector LoRA for Keye (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51780">#51780</a>) and Ultravox (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48215">#48215</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Vision encoders</strong>: ViT full CUDA graph for Kimi-K2.5 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50929">#50929</a>) and Ernie-4.5-VL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45254">#45254</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51461">#51461</a>), torch.compile for the Qwen3-VL encoder (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/40116">#40116</a>), and long-blocking H2D copies avoided in ViT (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51841">#51841</a>).</li>
<li><strong>MoE</strong>: extended EPLB support for Mistral Large 3 and additional MoE backends (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48355">#48355</a>), CuTe DSL skinny GEMM extended to GLM-5.2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49791">#49791</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding coverage</strong>: EAGLE3 support declared on KimiLinear (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52171">#52171</a>), Qwen3.6 dSpark acceptance coverage (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51310">#51310</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Correctness</strong>: MiniMax-M3 NVFP4 inference (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48929">#48929</a>) and compressed-tensors FP8 MoE SwiGLU params (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46845">#46845</a>), Gemma3n/Gemma4 variable-length audio batch padding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50958">#50958</a>), Gemma 4 compatibility with the upcoming Transformers version (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49797">#49797</a>), and a Qwen3-Omni crash on video without an audio track (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48420">#48420</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal performance</strong>: fused on-device multimodal preprocess normalization (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50411">#50411</a>), faster placeholder and token-match scanning (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50716">#50716</a>), and repeated prompt-update scans avoided (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51774">#51774</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Engine Core</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding</strong>: DSpark confidence-scheduled verification (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47808">#47808</a>), top-k DSpark Markov projection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49969">#49969</a>), DFlash2 with local convolution and a candidate selector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52816">#52816</a>), async scheduling auto-enabled for draft models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48341">#48341</a>), fused MTP trailing all-reduce with local-argmax draft tokens (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49793">#49793</a>), and an adaptive budget for speculative scheduled input tokens (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51725">#51725</a>).</li>
<li><strong>KV cache &amp; scheduling</strong>: per-request scheduling for MLA chunked context (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50613">#50613</a>), partial-tail prefix reuse with fine-grained prefix matching (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50507">#50507</a>), backend-published KV packing in the KV-cache layout refactor (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51612">#51612</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51704">#51704</a>), LIFO free-block reuse order restored when prefix caching is off (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51482">#51482</a>), and silent request skipping in priority scheduling fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49206">#49206</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Performance</strong>: continued elimination of GPU&lt;-&gt;CPU syncs on the execution path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51458">#51458</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51738">#51738</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52369">#52369</a>) now guarded by a CI sync check (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43107">#43107</a>), new JIT warmup infrastructure with predicate filtering (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49315">#49315</a>), the top-k/top-p Triton sampler launched with 8 warps (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51507">#51507</a>), detokenization skipped in offline beam search (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50333">#50333</a>), Mask Replay (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49577">#49577</a>), optimized long-context MLA cache gathers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51739">#51739</a>), and HF revisions resolved to a commit hash once per model load (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49990">#49990</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Hybrid/Mamba</strong>: prefix caching on by default (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50991">#50991</a>), the final part of the Mamba attention module refactor (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44857">#44857</a>), 3D-grid tiling of the state-copy Triton kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49436">#49436</a>), and Mamba alignment applied before encoder caps (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51603">#51603</a>).</li>
<li><strong>RL workflows</strong>: stateful trainer send over NCCL and sparse NCCL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50902">#50902</a>), <code>CuMemAllocator.discard()</code> for tag-selective GPU memory release (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52514">#52514</a>), level-2 sleep/wake/reload fixed with LoRA enabled (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/39935">#39935</a>), and rewritten weight-transfer docs with standardized examples (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51729">#51729</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Startup robustness</strong>: file:// rendezvous for single-node executors eliminates startup port races (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50999">#50999</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51652">#51652</a>), frontend processes are watched during engine startup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43417">#43417</a>), a <code>get_open_port()</code> livelock on DP-reserved ports was fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50965">#50965</a>), and NVML is no longer re-initialized on every device-capability check (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50393">#50393</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Hardware &amp; Performance</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>NVIDIA</strong>: FlashInfer XQA decode support on SM12x (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49718">#49718</a>), a CuTeDSL fused query kernel on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49792">#49792</a>), programmatic dependent launch for the DSA decode kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50230">#50230</a>), the native DSA decode path for MTP=3 on SM90 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52164">#52164</a>), GB10 fused-MoE FP8 tuning configs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52502">#52502</a>), and B12X dense linear backends (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52016">#52016</a>).</li>
<li><strong>AMD ROCm</strong>: torch 2.12 / triton 3.7 stack bump (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50607">#50607</a>), AITER and FP8 inference enabled on GFX120x (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43615">#43615</a>), DeepSeek-V4 on gfx11 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47017">#47017</a>), optimized Triton sparse-MLA decode on gfx950 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52212">#52212</a>), FlyDSL decode-attention kernel for 4-bit TurboQuant KV cache (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47896">#47896</a>) and an fp8 MQA logits kernel on gfx942 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49544">#49544</a>), a fused Kimi-K3 KDA decode kernel (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50654">#50654</a>), fused bf16→fp32 router GEMM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50268">#50268</a>), pinned memory on supported WSL2 kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50126">#50126</a>), and preshuffled sparse indexing for 16-token blocks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51216">#51216</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Intel XPU</strong>: a torch linear backend including blockwise GEMM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49664">#49664</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50826">#50826</a>), MXFP8 linear weights for the INC DeepSeek V4 model (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48476">#48476</a>), async-scheduling PP sampled-token broadcast overlapped with compute (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51650">#51650</a>), an XPU wheel added to the release pipeline (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52108">#52108</a>), tuned Mamba SSU configs for Arc Pro B70 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50534">#50534</a>), and UVA weight offloading fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51770">#51770</a>).</li>
<li><strong>CPU</strong>: an MLA backend so DeepSeek-V2/V3 can run on CPU (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49453">#49453</a>), a triton-cpu wheel (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52092">#52092</a>), GPTQ and AWQ enabled on s390x (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51148">#51148</a>) along with tcmalloc (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50841">#50841</a>), BF16 MoE routed through zentorch on AMD (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44201">#44201</a>), an unquantized MoE backend for Power (VSX) (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51624">#51624</a>), unquantized MoE migrated to the modular-kernel experts structure (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50133">#50133</a>), and the MXFP4 block scale folded in 2 instructions instead of 4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51583">#51583</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Large Scale Serving</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>E/P/D disaggregation</strong>: Model Runner V2 E/P/D support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/38390">#38390</a>), duplicate image preprocessing removed with GPU-side preprocessing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50390">#50390</a>), KV consumers may omit multimodal embeddings (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52697">#52697</a>), encoder-instance requests kept alive until their images are encoded (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50275">#50275</a>), and EC connector scheduler/worker metadata plumbing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49579">#49579</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49585">#49585</a>).</li>
<li><strong>KV offloading</strong>: disk offloading for SimpleCPUOffloadConnector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49644">#49644</a>), out-of-tree secondary tier managers via <code>module_path</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51007">#51007</a>), partial secondary-tier load results (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50321">#50321</a>), tiering offloading metrics (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48798">#48798</a>), data-parallel topology exposed to offloading backends (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51879">#51879</a>), a canonical CPU layout for parallelism-agnostic offload (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48414">#48414</a>), and quadratic ARC batch eviction avoided (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50992">#50992</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Mooncake</strong>: store group semantics (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44956">#44956</a>), tenant ID support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48069">#48069</a>), and official wheels in the Docker image (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51067">#51067</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Connectors</strong>: transfer mode (push/pull) included in the NIXL compatibility hash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50620">#50620</a>), a MoRIIO per-layer READ-completion barrier (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48534">#48534</a>), 2P2D wide-EP with mori-ep/mori-io at dp=ep=16 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45043">#45043</a>), and stale remote cleanup in the push connector (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50234">#50234</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Parallelism</strong>: EPLB balancedness calculation fixed and tested (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51813">#51813</a>), dense multinode DP rescope (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49212">#49212</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Quantization</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Online quantization</strong>: online MXFP4 support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49347">#49347</a>), online weight scales shared across TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49764">#49764</a>), precision preserved in online NVFP4 expert packing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50029">#50029</a>), and the online NVFP4 MoE kernel reused across reloads (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50074">#50074</a>).</li>
<li><strong>NVFP4</strong>: batch-invariant NVFP4 MoE via CUTLASS (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/40372">#40372</a>), KV 4-over-6 scale search (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45187">#45187</a>), CuTeDSL MoE with SwiGLU-OAI and ReLU2 activations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47106">#47106</a>), and out_dtype matched to the model dtype (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48861">#48861</a>).</li>
<li><strong>New kernels</strong>: block-wise scaled_mm (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49932">#49932</a>), DeepSeek-V4 AMD Quark NVFP4 with an emulation kernel (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47972">#47972</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Fixes</strong>: dynamic INT8 W8A8 MoE config no longer built as W8A16 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50833">#50833</a>) and a TritonExperts crash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51411">#51411</a>), MXFP4 conversion for FlashInfer CUTLASS (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51038">#51038</a>), fp32 weight scales and per-expert checkpoint mapping for MXFP4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51419">#51419</a>), and fused block-scale orientation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50727">#50727</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>API &amp; Frontend</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New capabilities</strong>: request priority parsed from an HTTP header (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51089">#51089</a>), session ID plumbing into requests (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48048">#48048</a>), <code>count_reasoning_tokens</code> in the streaming parser engine (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45802">#45802</a>), <code>content_parts</code> on <code>/inference/v1/generate</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51478">#51478</a>), <code>model</code> optional on all <code>/derender</code> request classes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51463">#51463</a>), output token IDs logged at DEBUG level (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52098">#52098</a>), and vLLM Recipes connected to native config-based deployment and benchmarking (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51308">#51308</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51878">#51878</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Rust frontend</strong>: a standalone renderer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50289">#50289</a>), gRPC multimodal image inference (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50368">#50368</a>), explicit data-parallel rank routing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51178">#51178</a>), RL lifecycle control (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51316">#51316</a>), dynamic tools from developer messages (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51144">#51144</a>), protobuf schemas published to Buf (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51276">#51276</a>), and MiniJinja upgraded to 2.22 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51235">#51235</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Anthropic API</strong>: 4xx returned for client-caused errors on <code>/v1/messages</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52246">#52246</a>), <code>disable_parallel_tool_use</code> preserved (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52021">#52021</a>), and stop sequences bounded (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51997">#51997</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Cohere</strong>: upstreamed parser fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51998">#51998</a>), stop sequences reported correctly (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51556">#51556</a>), and vectorized binary embedding bit-packing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52277">#52277</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Structured output</strong>: request stop tokens masked in xgrammar until the grammar terminates (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49227">#49227</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50595">#50595</a>), NUL bytes rejected in <code>structured_outputs.regex</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51796">#51796</a>), negative token IDs rejected as out-of-vocabulary (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51795">#51795</a>), and <code>VLLMValidationError</code> raised from validators (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52394">#52394</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Robustness</strong>: uvicorn signal handlers disabled instead of racing them (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50916">#50916</a>), a consolidated entrypoint exception handler (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52261">#52261</a>), 400 instead of 500 on non-object JSON bodies (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51654">#51654</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52528">#52528</a>), generation inputs bounded before expensive work (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51447">#51447</a>), and <code>cache_salt</code> now required to be non-empty (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50816">#50816</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Security</h2>
<ul>
<li>Fixed a DoS via sample-rate forgery that bypassed the audio decode duration guard (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49948">#49948</a>); the audio decode duration limit is now also enforced in NanoNemotronVL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50221">#50221</a>).</li>
<li>DeepStream classified as a GPU backend with pixel limits enforced (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50755">#50755</a>).</li>
<li><code>_load_ov2_processor</code> guarded with <code>resolve_trust_remote_code</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52952">#52952</a>).</li>
<li>Documentation now warns that <code>--api-key</code> does not gate all endpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51999">#51999</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Dependencies</h2>
<ul>
<li>Transformers 5.15.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51668">#51668</a>), huggingface-hub 1.27.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51422">#51422</a>), fastsafetensors upgrade (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50827">#50827</a>).</li>
<li>ROCm: torch 2.12, triton 3.7, torchaudio, torchvision (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50607">#50607</a>).</li>
<li>Runtime image upgraded to Ubuntu 24.04, picking up rdma-core &gt; 44 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51058">#51058</a>).</li>
<li>DeepGEMM pinned to the deepseek-ai nv_dev tip (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52035">#52035</a>), DeepEP pinned by full commit hash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52028">#52028</a>), FlashAttention 3 built with the torch stable API (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49599">#49599</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Breaking Changes &amp; Deprecations</h2>
<ul>
<li>bitsandbytes support is now an out-of-tree plugin (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43529">#43529</a>).</li>
<li>The deprecated <code>calculate_kv_scales</code> runtime KV scale calculation was removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49389">#49389</a>).</li>
<li><code>override_attention_dtype</code> was removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48684">#48684</a>).</li>
<li><code>reasoning_content</code> output removal is documented as a breaking client change (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50624">#50624</a>).</li>
<li>KV offload tiering metrics renamed from <code>kv_offload_tiering_block_{queries,hits}</code> to <code>..._chunk_...</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52812">#52812</a>).</li>
<li>MoE legacy code removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51078">#51078</a>).</li>
</ul>

<li><a class="user-mention notranslate" href="https://github.com/iwannagotobed">@iwannagotobed</a> made their first contribution in <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51664">#51664</a></li>

<li><a class="user-mention notranslate" href="https://github.com/jimmy-adams">@jimmy-adams</a> made their first contribution in <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47972">#47972</a></li>
<li><a class="user-mention notranslate" href="https://github.com/jyan-R">@jyan-R</a> made their first contribution in <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52311">#52311</a></li>

### [A decade of Rustls](https://lwn.net/Articles/1093391/)

**来源**: LWN.net (Linux Weekly News)

**摘要**: <p>Joe Birr-Pixton has written <a href="https://rustls.dev/blog/2026-09-08-a-decade-of-rustls/">a blog post</a>
reflecting on a decade of the Rustls TLS-library project and looking ahead to
the upcoming&#160;0.24 release and an eventual&#160;1.0 release.</p>

<blockquote class="bq">
<p>Rustls began with <a href="https://github.com/rustls/rustls/commit/4460bbe15b4df1f6e6efea4f9f06fbd0fb278077">a
first commit</a> on May&#160;2,&#160;2016. Progress was quick: a month later, on June&#160;5,
it could interoperate with most sites on the web. The first release,&#160;0.1.0,
followed on August&#160;27,&#160;2016 – less than four months after the first commit.</p>

<p>[...] From the&#160;0.1.0 release, the project moved through a long series of
releases over the following eight years, building out functionality, hardening
and refining the API. That sequence of release lines culminated in&#160;0.23,
released on February&#160;29,&#160;2024.</p>

<p>The&#160;0.23 release line has been a stable one: in the time since, it has
seen&#160;43 non-breaking releases. That stability didn't come with
stagnation. The&#160;0.23 line delivered a wide range of important features,
including a FIPS-certified cryptography option, certificate compression,
Encrypted ClientHello, post-quantum cryptography, and performance
improvements.</p>
</blockquote>

---

### [LibreOffice Base survey results](https://lwn.net/Articles/1093388/)

**来源**: LWN.net (Linux Weekly News)

**摘要**: <p>Heiko Tietze has <a href="https://design.blog.documentfoundation.org/2026/09/03/results-from-the-survey-about-libreoffice-base/">published
a blog post</a> summarizing the results of a <a href="https://design.blog.documentfoundation.org/2026/07/30/please-participate-in-a-survey-about-libreoffice-base/">recent
survey</a> about the use of LibreOffice's database application, Base. 455 people
participated in the survey, including more than 330 who use Base on Linux, with
use cases ranging from maintaining records of personal media such as CDs or DVDs
to use enterprise-resource planning (ERP) and finance. Of course, users had many
ideas how to improve the application:</p>

<blockquote class="bq">
<p>The majority asks for improvements to the user interface with less
clutter and a more attractive design. The workflow and user experience should
become either simplified or more powerful, depending on the expertise and the
scenario. For example, an elaborate search function is something that
many people expect. [...]</p>

<p>Almost the same number of answers requests bug fixes, improvements to
stability, and better performance. Issues with queries, forms, and reports were
mentioned equally often. In this regard, many replies suggest to remove the Java
dependencies.</p>
</blockquote>

<p></p>

### [[$] Typst makes big strides](https://lwn.net/Articles/1092993/)

**来源**: LWN.net (Linux Weekly News)

**摘要**: <a href="https://typst.app">Typst</a> is a system for typesetting documents
into various formats: PDF, SVG, PNG, and, in progress, HTML. It is adept at
handling technical material, and is often considered to be an eventual <a href="https://www.latex-project.org/">LaTeX</a> replacement. We last  <a href="https://lwn.net/Articles/1037577/">looked
in on Typst</a> a year ago, when it had reached version 0.13.  A new version,
0.15, was <a href="https://typst.app/blog/2026/typst-0.15/">released</a> in
June with lots of  <a href="https://typst.app/docs/changelog/0.15.0/">new features</a>, including support for variable fonts,
MathML, multiple bibliographies, and more. Typst is free, Apache-2.0-licensed software, programmed in Rust.

Extra cores do not help until you create more threads. However, if you include the header &#60;threads.h&#62;, you can pass a function to thrd_create, and wait for it with thrd_join. #include &#60;threads.h&#62; #include &#60;stdio.h&#62; int worker(void *arg) { printf("hello from thread %d\n", *(int &#8230; <a class="more-link" href="https://lemire.me/blog/2026/09/09/a-quick-overview-of-atomics-in-c/">Continue reading <span class="screen-reader-text">A quick overview of atomics in C</span></a>

## Systems

### [Some more things about Django I've been enjoying](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/07/21/more-nice-django-things/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! I&rsquo;m on a funny journey right now where I&rsquo;m trying to learn how to make
websites in a sort of 2010 style, where I have an SQL database and render some
HTML on the backend.</p>
<p>It&rsquo;s kind of an interesting journey because it doesn&rsquo;t necessarily feel &ldquo;easy&rdquo;
to me to make websites in this way: I never learned how to do it in the 2000s or
2010s, and there&rsquo;s a lot I need to learn.</p>
<p>So here are some Django features that make building this kind of site feel
more achievable than when I was trying and failing to use Go&rsquo;s standard library
or Flask. And I&rsquo;ll talk about a couple of issues with Django I&rsquo;ve run into.</p>
<h3 id="why-learn-to-make-websites-like-it-s-2010">why learn to make websites like it&rsquo;s 2010?</h3>
<p>Previously the toolkit I felt confident with for making websites was:</p>
<ul>
<li>static site generators (like for this blog)</li>
<li>static sites that do some fun stuff with Javascript (like this <a href="https://sql-playground.wizardzines.com/">sql playground</a>)</li>
<li>simple Vue.js single page apps with either a Lambda as a backend or a Go backend (like <a href="https://messwithdns.net/">mess with dns</a>)</li>
</ul>
<p>I really liked this frontend-heavy approach for these super simple applications
but when I started thinking about making something with a lot of different pages
(instead of literally just one page), I didn&rsquo;t feel so excited about the options
I saw that involved a lot of frontend code. So I figured I&rsquo;d try the backend.</p>
<p>Writing a backend-focused site that uses as little JS as possible feels
the same to me in a way as writing a single-page JS website that does as little
on the backend as possible, even though they might seem like opposites. In both
cases I&rsquo;m just trying to keep as much of the logic as possible in one place.</p>
<p>Now for some thoughts about Django!</p>
<h3 id="i-m-enjoying-query-builders">I&rsquo;m enjoying query builders</h3>
<p>I learned that I can define a &ldquo;query set&rdquo; class in Django with a bunch of
methods with different <code>WHERE</code> statements I might want to use while constructing
a query:</p>
<p>Here&rsquo;s how I use it in my view code once I&rsquo;ve defined what all the methods mean:</p>
<pre><code>Events.objects.approved()
  .for_tab(tab)
  .with_festivals(tab_params.festival_slugs)
  .is_free(tab_params.free)
  .is_outdoors(tab_params.outdoors)
</code></pre>
<p>and here&rsquo;s how I define the methods:</p>
<pre><code>class EventQuerySet(SearchableQuerySetMixin, models.QuerySet):
    def approved(self):
        return self.filter(approved_at__isnull=False)

    def future(self):
        today = timezone.localdate()
        return self.filter(end__gt=self._midnight(today))

    def with_tags(self, tags):
        if tags:
            return self.filter(tags__name__in=tags).distinct()
        return self
</code></pre>
<p>The syntax for defining the filters isn&rsquo;t my favourite, but I spend most of my
time just using the methods, and it feels super readable and nice to use, and
it makes me want to look into other query builder libraries in the future. In
the past I thought &ldquo;I know SQL, who needs a query builder?&rdquo;, but this kind of
structure does make it really nice to read.</p>
<p>I found an example of someone who <a href="https://github.com/lemon24/reader/blob/15121f667a6f2e388f0072a3fcd715f533883899/src/reader/_sql_utils.py">wrote their own small query builder in Python</a>
that I want to read later to think about whether I would enjoy using a more
minimal version of this.</p>
<h3 id="the-template-filters-are-awesome">the template filters are awesome</h3>
<p>There are a bunch of little quality of life
<a href="https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/templates/builtins/">filters available in Django templates</a> that are super useful for generating HTML. The ones I&rsquo;ve used so far are:</p>
<ul>
<li>translating plain text URLs into links, or line breaks into <code>&lt;br&gt;</code> (<code>{{ event.description|urlize|linebreaksbr }} </code>)</li>
<li>formatting dates (<code>{{ row.date|date:&quot;M j&quot; }}</code>)</li>
<li><code>json_script</code>, which takes a Python dictionary and automatically converts it to JSON and inserts it into the HTML as a <code>&lt;script&gt;</code> tag in a safe way</li>
</ul>
<p>These are all small things individually but I feel like it makes a big difference somehow to just have them available.</p>
<h3 id="querystring-is-cool"><code>querystring</code> is cool</h3>
<p>I think my favourite template filter is <code>querystring</code>: in this site sometimes
we use filters like <code>?date=2026-06-01</code> to decide what&rsquo;s displayed. <code>querystring</code>
that will make a link to the same query string with one change, like this to
link to the previous date:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;a href=&quot;{% querystring date=nav.prev_date%}&quot;&gt;
</code></pre>
<p>Or to remove the <code>outdoors</code> parameter:</p>
<pre><code>&lt;a href=&quot;{% querystring outdoors=None %}&quot;&gt;
</code></pre>
<h3 id="automatic-database-migrations-are-still-great">automatic database migrations are still great</h3>
<p>I still really love Django&rsquo;s automatic database system. It&rsquo;s amazing to be
able to just edit a model to add a new field or whatever, and then Django
automatically generates the migration.</p>
<p>So far we have done 19 database migrations and I think there will probably be
more! It makes a huge difference for me to be able to just easily change the
database as my understanding of the problem changes.</p>
<h3 id="i-do-not-want-to-organize-my-code-with-inheritance">I do not want to organize my code with inheritance</h3>
<p>Django&rsquo;s documentation sometimes offers the option of using class-based views
and inheritance to organize the code in your views. For example I have four
views that share a lot of code, and I could use inheritance to manage that by
defining some kind of parent class and then having my other views inherit from
it.</p>
<p>I tried it out and I did not enjoy the experience of using inheritance to
share code between views. I switched to using functions instead, sort of how
<a href="https://spookylukey.github.io/django-views-the-right-way/">this post</a> advocates, and that was a lot more straightforward.
I&rsquo;ve never had a good experience using inheritance in Python and I don&rsquo;t think
I&rsquo;ll try to use it again.</p>
<p>But I don&rsquo;t mind using inheritance to use the interfaces Django itself provides: for
example if I want to define a query set I need to write something like
<code>class EventQuerySet(SearchableQuerySetMixin, models.QuerySet)</code>.
I don&rsquo;t think too hard about it and it seems to work.</p>
<p>(as a meta comment: I&rsquo;ve been working on talking about my programming opinions
by just saying &ldquo;THING does not feel good to me, I prefer OTHER THING instead&rdquo;.
That post I linked to says that function-based views are the &ldquo;right way&rdquo;. I&rsquo;m
not very invested in whether it&rsquo;s &ldquo;right&rdquo;, but it&rsquo;s validating to know that
other people feel similarly to me about inheritance)</p>
<h3 id="i-don-t-know-how-to-think-about-django-performance">I don&rsquo;t know how to think about Django performance</h3>
<p>At some point the LLM scrapers discovered our site, and started sending us maybe 10
requests per second. I blocked them which is working for now, but it made me
think about what the site&rsquo;s capacity is. I&rsquo;m used to writing Go backends where
the performance situation is pretty straightforward (usually everything is just
fast enough), and a Django site is very different.</p>
<p>Some light load testing (with (<code>ab -n 1000 -c 1</code>) shows that right now we can
serve about 2-3 requests per second (on a ~$10/month VM).</p>
<p>It&rsquo;s tempting for me to go down a rabbit hole where I do a bunch of profiling to
figure out what&rsquo;s slow and try to make it faster
(there&rsquo;s <a href="https://github.com/benfred/py-spy">py-spy</a> for that, and py-spy is great and super easy to use, and profiling is fun!)
But I really don&rsquo;t understand what I should expect in terms of performance from
a Django site and how I should be thinking about at a higher level.</p>
<p>Some things I haven&rsquo;t figured out yet:</p>
<ul>
<li>If I have a site that&rsquo;s going to be getting occasional bursts of traffic, do I want to be able to scale up?</li>
<li>Do I want to design the site so that more things can be cached? (and do I really have to? caches are so annoying to get right!)</li>
<li>The <a href="https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/topics/performance/">django performance docs</a> say that Jinja is faster for templating, do I want to think about switching templating systems?</li>
<li>Those docs also say &ldquo;{% block %} is faster than using {% include %}&rdquo;, I wonder if it&rsquo;s a big difference and if so why</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="template-caching-might-be-important">template caching might be important</h3>
<p>I think one thing I&rsquo;m learning about Django is that because it&rsquo;s a Framework
(tm), it&rsquo;s easy to accidentally misconfigure it. For example, when I was
thinking about why my site was slow just now, I read the
<a href="https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/topics/performance/">django performance docs</a> and I noticed
a comment saying:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Enabling the cached template loader often improves performance drastically, as
it avoids compiling each template every time it needs to be rendered.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>When I&rsquo;d done CPU profiling I&rsquo;d noticed that it was spending a lot of time
rendering templates! Maybe this could help me!</p>
<p>Clicking through the link, I saw that the cached template loader was supposed to
be on by default, but I&rsquo;d turned it off by accident while trying to do something
else. I think this &ldquo;I turned off the cached template loader by default&rdquo;
things is an example of how I still find the django settings file to be pretty
confusing and difficult. I guess I should just be careful when I go in there.</p>
<p>After turning on template caching, it seems like the site can now pretty easily
handle 12 requests per second or so without using all of the CPU. I have not
carefully benchmarked the before and after but it seems like it&rsquo;s made a pretty
big difference.</p>
<p>One thing that&rsquo;s been surprising to me about Django performance is that I&rsquo;ve
always heard the advice &ldquo;if you have a performance problem, check your database
queries! Maybe add an index!&rdquo;. But I&rsquo;ve been running into a variety of
performance issues (like this template caching thing) that are not because of
slow queries, so instead it&rsquo;s been more useful for me so far to start by running
a CPU profile. And since I&rsquo;m using SQLite, any slow database query problem will
show up on the CPU profile anyway.</p>
<p>Anyway I don&rsquo;t want to get too far into site performance. Like I said it&rsquo;s easy
for me to get interested in profiling, but actually I know a lot about profiling
and it&rsquo;s not the most important thing for me to learn about.</p>
<h3 id="that-s-all-for-now">that&rsquo;s all for now!</h3>
<p>I might say more about what I&rsquo;m enjoying (or having a hard time with!) about
Django later. Trying to write some shorter blog posts recently.</p>

---

### [Learning a few things about running SQLite](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/07/17/learning-about-running-sqlite/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! I&rsquo;ve been working on a Django site recently, and I decided to use SQLite
as the database.
When I was getting started with using SQLite as database for a website I read a <a href="https://alldjango.com/articles/definitive-guide-to-using-django-sqlite-in-production">bunch</a>
of blog posts about how it is totally fine to use SQLite in production for a
small site and I think it <em>is</em> totally fine, but what I did not fully appreciate
is that SQLite is still a database, databases are complicated, and I do not know
a lot about operating databases.</p>
<p>So here are a couple of small things I&rsquo;ve been learning about running SQLite. This
is the 4th website I&rsquo;ve used SQLite for, and I think this one is harder because
with the power of the Django ORM I&rsquo;ve been making the database do more work than
I was previously without Django.</p>
<p>I started by turning on WAL mode like all the blog posts said to do and hoping for the best.</p>
<h3 id="analyze-is-apparently-important"><code>ANALYZE</code> is apparently important</h3>
<p>Today I was running a query (using <a href="https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html">SQLite&rsquo;s FTS5</a> for
full-text search) on a table with 4000 rows and it took 5 seconds. That seemed
wrong to me: computers are fast!</p>
<p>It turned out that what I needed to do was to run <a href="https://sqlite.org/lang_analyze.html"><code>ANALYZE</code></a>!
Immediately the problem query went from taking 5 seconds to like 0.05 seconds
(or some other number small enough that I didn&rsquo;t care to investigate further).
I still don&rsquo;t know exactly what went wrong in the query plan,
but my best guess is that it was some sort of <a href="https://accidentallyquadratic.tumblr.com/">accidentally quadratic</a> thing.</p>
<p><code>ANALYZE</code> generates &ldquo;statistics&rdquo; (I guess about the number of rows in each table? and presumably other things?)
so that the query planner can make better choices.</p>
<p>Maybe one day I&rsquo;ll learn to read a query plan.</p>
<h3 id="cleaning-up-the-database-is-tricky">cleaning up the database is tricky</h3>
<p>Occasionally I&rsquo;ve run into situations where I accidentally put a bunch of rows
in my database that I don&rsquo;t want to be there (for example completed tasks from
<a href="https://github.com/RealOrangeOne/django-tasks-db">django-tasks-db</a>), and I want to clean them up.</p>
<p>What&rsquo;s happened to me a few times in this case is:</p>
<ol>
<li>I run some kind of command to clean up the rows</li>
<li>The command takes more than 5 seconds, since there are a lot of rows (though I still have some questions about why these DELETE statements are so slow honestly, maybe there&rsquo;s a bunch of Python code running inside a transaction, I&rsquo;m not sure)</li>
<li>One of the other workers tries to write the database while this is happening, and times out after 5 seconds (I have a timeout of 5 seconds set)</li>
<li>The worker crashes because it couldn&rsquo;t write to the database and the VM shuts down</li>
</ol>
<p>My approach so far has been to just do these cleanup operations in small batches
so that I don&rsquo;t need to do database queries that take more than 5 seconds to
run. This whole experience has given me more of an appreciation for why someone
might want to use a &ldquo;real&rdquo; database like Postgres which can have more than one
writer at the same time though.</p>
<p>Maybe in the future I&rsquo;ll just take the site down for scheduled maintenance
instead when I need to do this kind of thing, but I haven&rsquo;t figured out a
workflow for that yet.</p>
<h3 id="no-notes-on-performance-of-orm-queries-yet">no notes on performance of ORM queries yet</h3>
<p>So far I&rsquo;ve been using Django&rsquo;s ORM to make any query I want without paying any
attention at all to query performance and it&rsquo;s mostly been going okay other
than the <code>ANALYZE</code> thing. The database is pretty small (maybe 10000 rows?) and
I expect it to stay pretty small forever, so I&rsquo;m hoping that that plan will
keep working.</p>
<h3 id="backing-up-sqlite">backing up sqlite</h3>
<p>I&rsquo;ve done SQLite backups a couple of ways. I don&rsquo;t think I&rsquo;ve actually tested
restoring from my backups but I do usually try to monitor them with a dead man&rsquo;s
switch.</p>
<p><strong>way 1: restic</strong></p>
<pre><code>sqlite3 /data/calendar.db &quot;VACUUM INTO '/tmp/calendar.sqlite'&quot;
gzip /tmp/calendar.sqlite

# Upload backup to S3
# Sometimes the backup gets OOM killed and so it stays locked, do an unlock
restic -r s3://s3.amazonaws.com/some_bucket/ unlock
# Do the backup &amp; prune old backups
restic -r s3://s3.amazonaws.com/some_bucket/ backup /tmp/calendar.sqlite.gz
restic -r s3://s3.amazonaws.com/some_bucket/ snapshots
restic -r s3://s3.amazonaws.com/some_bucket/ forget -l 1 -H 6 -d 2 -w 2 -m 2 -y 2
restic -r s3://s3.amazonaws.com/some_bucket/ prune
</code></pre>
<p><strong>way 2: <a href="https://litestream.io/">litestream</a></strong></p>
<p>I started trying out Litestream recently because I felt like doing incremental backups might
be more efficient: my restic backups were sometimes getting OOM killed, and I
was a bit tired of it. Basically I just write a config file and run:</p>
<pre><code>litestream replicate -config litestream.yml
</code></pre>
<p>I set <code>retention: 400h</code> in my config file in an attempt to
retain some amount of history of the database but I have no idea if it works.</p>
<p>I&rsquo;ve been backing up to AWS, which is always a pain because it&rsquo;s annoying to
navigate the AWS console to generate credentials. Maybe one day I&rsquo;ll move away
to some other S3-compatible alternative.</p>
<h3 id="you-can-use-multiple-databases">you can use multiple databases</h3>
<p>My current project only has one database, but one trick I used with
<a href="https://messwithdns.net/">Mess with DNS</a> was to split the tables into three separate
database files because I didn&rsquo;t actually need my tables to be in the same db. I think it was helpful.</p>
<p>Mess with DNS has been running on SQLite for 4 years now (since 2022) and it&rsquo;s
been great, I think the move from Postgres was a great choice for that project.</p>
<h3 id="that-s-all">that&rsquo;s all!</h3>
<p>It&rsquo;s always kind of fun to see how long it takes me to learn sort of basic
things about the technologies I&rsquo;m using. I think I used SQLite for a web project
for the first time in 2022 and I only learned that <code>ANALYZE</code> existed today!
I imagine in a year or two I&rsquo;ll be learning about some other very basic feature.</p>
<h3 id="some-references">some references</h3>
<p>Some blog posts I&rsquo;ve looked at, other than the official docs:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://alldjango.com/articles/definitive-guide-to-using-django-sqlite-in-production">The definitive guide to using Django with SQLite in production</a></li>
<li><a href="https://gist.github.com/phiresky/978d8e204f77feaa0ab5cca08d2d5b27">a gist on sqlite performance tuning</a></li>
</ul>

---

### [Moving away from Tailwind, and learning to structure my CSS](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/05/15/moving-away-from-tailwind--and-learning-to-structure-my-css-/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! 8 years ago, I <a href="https://jvns.ca/blog/2018/11/01/tailwind--write-css-without-the-css/">wrote excitedly about discovering Tailwind</a>.</p>
<p>At that time I really had no idea how to structure my CSS code and given the
choice between a pile of complete chaos and Tailwind, I was really happy to choose
Tailwind. It helped me make a lot of tiny sites!</p>
<p>I spent the last week or so migrating a couple of sites away from Tailwind and
towards more semantic HTML + vanilla CSS, and it was SO fun and SO interesting,
so here are some things I learned!</p>
<p>As usual I&rsquo;m not a full-time frontend developer and so all of my CSS learning
has happened in fits and starts over many years.</p>
<h3 id="it-turns-out-tailwind-taught-me-a-lot">it turns out Tailwind taught me a lot</h3>
<p>When I started thinking about structuring CSS, I was intimidated at first: I&rsquo;m
not very good at structuring my CSS! But then I started reading blog posts
talking about how to structure CSS (like <a href="https://www.miriamsuzanne.com/2022/09/06/layers/">A whole cascade of layers</a> or <a href="https://jacobb.nyc/writing/how-i-write-css-in-2024">How I write CSS in 2024</a>)
and I realized a couple of things:</p>
<ol>
<li>Every CSS code base has a bunch of different things going on (layouts! fonts! colours! common components!)</li>
<li>It&rsquo;s extremely useful to have systems or guidelines to manage each of those things, otherwise things descend into chaos</li>
<li>Tailwind has systems for some of these, and I already know those systems! Maybe I can imitate the systems I like!</li>
</ol>
<p>For example, Tailwind has:</p>
<ul>
<li>a reset stylesheet</li>
<li>a <a href="https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/05/04/css-colour-palettes/">colour palette</a></li>
<li>a <a href="https://v2.tailwindcss.com/docs/font-size">font scale</a></li>
</ul>
<h3 id="the-systems-i-m-going-to-talk-about">the systems I&rsquo;m going to talk about</h3>
<p>I&rsquo;m going to talk about a few aspects of my CSS codebase and my thoughts so far
what kind of rules I want to impose on the codebase for each one. Some of them
are copied from Tailwind and some aren&rsquo;t.</p>
<ol>
<li>reset</li>
<li>components</li>
<li>colours</li>
<li>font sizes</li>
<li>utility classes</li>
<li>the base</li>
<li>spacing</li>
<li>responsive design</li>
<li>the build system</li>
</ol>
<h3 id="1-reset">1. reset</h3>
<p>I just copied Tailwind&rsquo;s &ldquo;<a href="https://v2.tailwindcss.com/docs/preflight">preflight styles</a>&rdquo;
by going into <code>tailwind.css</code> and copying the first 200 lines or so.</p>
<p>I noticed that I&rsquo;ve developed a relationship with Tailwind&rsquo;s CSS reset over time,
for example Tailwind sets <code>box-sizing: border-box</code> on every element (which means
that an element&rsquo;s width includes its padding and border):</p>
<pre><code>* { box-sizing: border-box; }
</code></pre>
<p>I think it would be a real adjustment for me to switch to writing CSS without
these, and I&rsquo;m sure there are lots of other things in the Tailwind reset (like
<code>html {line-height: 1.5;}</code>) that I&rsquo;m subconsciously used to and don&rsquo;t even realize are
there.</p>
<h3 id="2-components">2. components</h3>
<p>This next part is the bulk of the CSS!</p>
<p>The idea here is to organize CSS by &ldquo;components&rdquo;, in a way that&rsquo;s spiritually
related to Vue or React components. (though there might not actually be any Javascript at all in the site)</p>
<p>Basically the idea is that:</p>
<ol>
<li>Each &ldquo;component&rdquo; has a unique class</li>
<li>The CSS for one component never overrides the CSS for any other component</li>
<li>Each component has its own CSS file</li>
</ol>
<p>So editing the CSS for one component won&rsquo;t mysteriously break something in
another component. And probably like 80% of the CSS that I would actually want
to change is in various component files, so if I&rsquo;m editing a 100-line component,
I just have to think about those 100 lines. It&rsquo;s way easier for me to think
about.</p>
<p>For example, this HTML might be the <code>.zine</code> &ldquo;component&rdquo;.</p>
<pre><code>&lt;figure class=&quot;zine horizontal&quot;&gt;
    &lt;img src=&quot;whatever.jpg&quot;&gt;
&lt;/figure&gt;
</code></pre>
<p>And the CSS looks something like this, using nested selectors:</p>
<pre><code>.zine {
  ...
  &amp;.horizontal {
    ...
  }
  &amp;.vertical {
    ...
  }
  &amp;:hover {
    ...
  }
}
</code></pre>
<p>I haven&rsquo;t done anything programmatic (like web components or
<a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@scope">@scope</a>)
that ensures that components won&rsquo;t interfere with each other, but just having a
convention and trying my best already feels like a big improvement.</p>
<p>Next: conventions to maintain some consistency across the site and keep these
components in line with each other!</p>
<h3 id="3-colours">3. colours</h3>
<p><code>colours.css</code> has a bunch of variables like this which I can use as necessary.
Colour is really hard and I didn&rsquo;t want to revisit my use of colour in this
refactor, so I left this alone.</p>
<p>The only guideline I&rsquo;m trying to enforce here is that all colours used in the
site are listed in this file.</p>
<pre><code>:root {
  --pink: #fea0c2;
  --pink-light: #F9B9B9;
  --red: #f91a55;
  --orange: rgb(222, 117, 31);
  ...
}
</code></pre>
<h3 id="4-font-sizes">4. font sizes</h3>
<p>One thing I appreciated about Tailwind was that if I wanted to set a font
size, I could just think &ldquo;hm, I want the text to be big&rdquo;, write <code>text-lg</code>, and
be done with it! And maybe if it&rsquo;s not big enough I&rsquo;d use <code>xl</code> or <code>2xl</code> instead.
No trying to remember whether I&rsquo;m using <code>em</code> or <code>px</code> or <code>rem</code>.</p>
<p>So I defined a bunch of variables, taken from Tailwind, like this:</p>
<pre><code>  --size-xs: 0.75rem;
  --line-height-xs: 1rem;

  --size-sm: 0.875rem;
  --line-height-sm: 1.25rem;
</code></pre>
<p>Then if I want to set a font size, I can do it like this. It&rsquo;s a little more
verbose than Tailwind but I&rsquo;m happy with it for now.</p>
<pre><code>h3 {
  font-size: var(--size-lg);
  line-height: var(--line-height-lg);
}
</code></pre>
<h3 id="5-utilities">5. utilities</h3>
<p>There are some things like buttons that appear in many different components.
I&rsquo;m calling these &ldquo;utilities&rdquo;.</p>
<p>I copied some utility classes from Tailwind (like <code>.sr-only</code> for things that
should only appear for screenreader users).</p>
<p>This section is pretty small and I try to be careful about making changes here.</p>
<h3 id="6-the-base">6. the base</h3>
<p>&ldquo;base&rdquo; styles are styles that apply across the whole site that I chose myself. I
have to keep this section really small because I&rsquo;m not confident enough to
enforce a lot of styles across the whole site. These are the only two I feel
okay about right now, and I might change the <code>&lt;section&gt;</code> one:</p>
<pre><code>/* put a 950px column in the middle of each &lt;section&gt; */
section {
  --inner-width: 950px;
  padding: 3rem max(1rem, (100% - var(--inner-width))/2);
}

a {
  color: var(--orange);
}
</code></pre>
<p>I think for the base styles it&rsquo;s going to be easiest for me to work kind of
bottom up &ndash; first start with almost nothing in the base styles, and then move
some styles from the components into base styles as I identify common things
I want.</p>
<h3 id="7-spacing">7. spacing</h3>
<p>I haven&rsquo;t completely worked out an approach to managing padding and margins yet.
I&rsquo;m definitely trying to be more principled than how I was doing it in Tailwind
though, where I would just haphazardly put padding and margins everywhere until
it looked the way I wanted.</p>
<p>Right now I&rsquo;m working towards making the outer layout components in charge of
spacing as much as possible. For example if I have a <code>&lt;section&gt;</code> with a bunch of
children that I want to have space between them, I might use this to space the
children evenly:</p>
<pre><code>section &gt; *+* {
  margin-top: 1rem;
}
</code></pre>
<p>Some inspiration blog posts:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://piccalil.li/blog/my-favourite-3-lines-of-css/">the owl selector</a></li>
<li><a href="https://kyleshevlin.com/no-outer-margin/">&ldquo;no outer margin&rdquo;</a></li>
</ul>
<h3 id="8-responsive-design-use-more-grid">8. responsive design: use more grid!</h3>
<p>The way I was doing responsive design in Tailwind was to use a lot of media
queries. Tailwind has this <code>md:text-xl</code> syntax that means &ldquo;apply the <code>text-xl</code>
style at sizes <code>md</code> or larger&rdquo;.</p>
<p>I&rsquo;m trying something pretty different now, which is to make more flexible CSS
grid layouts that don&rsquo;t need as many breakpoints. This is hard but it&rsquo;s really
interesting to learn about what&rsquo;s possible with grid, and it&rsquo;s a good example of
something that I don&rsquo;t think is possible with Tailwind.</p>
<p>For example, I&rsquo;ve been learning about how to use <code>auto-fit</code> to automatically use
2 columns on a big screen and 1 column on a small screen like this:</p>
<pre><code>  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 400px), max-content));
  justify-content: center;
</code></pre>
<p>I also used <a href="https://wizardzines.com/comics/css-grid-areas/"><code>grid-template-areas</code></a> a lot which is an amazing feature that I don&rsquo;t think you can use with Tailwind.</p>
<p>Some inspiration:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://css-tricks.com/a-responsive-grid-layout-with-no-media-queries/">A responsive grid layout with no media queries</a> from CSS Tricks</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="9-the-build-system-esbuild">9. the build system: esbuild</h3>
<p>In development, I don&rsquo;t need a build system: CSS now has both built in import statements, like this:</p>
<pre><code>@import &quot;reset.css&quot;;
@import &quot;typography.css&quot;;
@import &quot;colors.css&quot;;
</code></pre>
<p>and built in nested selectors, like this:</p>
<pre><code>.page {
  h2 { ...}
}
</code></pre>
<p>If I want, I can use <code>esbuild</code> to bundle the CSS file for production. That looks something like this.</p>
<pre><code>esbuild style.css --bundle --loader:.svg=dataurl  --loader:.woff2=file --outfile=/tmp/out.css
</code></pre>
<p>Even though I usually avoid using CSS and JS build systems, I don&rsquo;t mind using esbuild
(which I <a href="https://jvns.ca/blog/2021/11/15/esbuild-vue/">wrote about in 2021 here</a>)
because it&rsquo;s based on web standards and because it&rsquo;s a static Go binary.</p>
<h3 id="why-migrate-away-from-tailwind">why migrate away from Tailwind?</h3>
<p>A few people asked why I was migrating away from Tailwind. A few factors that
contributed are:</p>
<ul>
<li>Tailwind has become much more reliant on a build system since 2018, I think it&rsquo;s
impossible (?) to use newer versions of Tailwind without using a build system.
So I&rsquo;ve been using Tailwind v2 for years. (there&rsquo;s also <a href="https://litewindcss.com/">litewind</a> apparently)</li>
<li>It&rsquo;s always been true that you&rsquo;re supposed to use Tailwind with a build
system, but I&rsquo;ve never really done that, so I have 2.8MB <code>tailwind.min.css</code>
files (270K gzipped) in a lot of my projects and it feels a little silly.</li>
<li>I&rsquo;m a lot better at CSS than I was when I started using Tailwind</li>
<li>Ultimately Tailwind is limiting: if you want to do Weird Stuff in your CSS,
it&rsquo;s not always possible with Tailwind. Those limits can be extremely useful
(a lot of this post is about me reimplementing some of Tailwind&rsquo;s limits!) but
at this point I&rsquo;d like to be able to pick and choose.</li>
<li>I ended up with sites that mixed both vanilla CSS and Tailwind in the same
project and that was not fun to maintain</li>
<li>I got curious about what writing more semantic HTML would feel like.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="css-features-i-m-curious-about">CSS features I&rsquo;m curious about</h3>
<p>While doing this I learned about a lot of CSS features that I didn&rsquo;t use but am
curious about learning about one day:</p>
<ul>
<li><code>@layer</code> (from <a href="https://www.miriamsuzanne.com/2022/09/06/layers/">A Whole Cascade of Layers</a>)</li>
<li><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@scope">@scope</a>) (especially <a href="https://drafts.csswg.org/css-cascade-6/#example-463550a5">this example of how to use @scope in a &ldquo;component&rdquo; CSS design from the specification!</a>)</li>
<li><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Guides/Containment/Container_queries">container queries</a></li>
<li><a href="https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Guides/Grid_layout/Subgrid">subgrid</a></li>
</ul>
<h3 id="one-last-reason-i-moved-away-from-tailwind">one last reason I moved away from Tailwind</h3>
<p>I&rsquo;ve been talking a lot in this post about what I learned from using Tailwind,
and that&rsquo;s all true.</p>
<p>But I read this post 3 years ago called <a href="https://thoughtbot.com/blog/tailwind-and-the-femininity-of-css">Tailwind and the Femininity of CSS</a>
that really stuck with me. I honestly probably started out with an attitude
towards CSS a little like that post describes:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>They’ve heard it’s simple, so they assume it’s easy. But then when they try to
use it, it doesn’t work. It must be the fault of the language, because they know
that they are smart, and this is supposed to be easy.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>But in the last 10 years I&rsquo;ve learned to really love and respect CSS as a
technology.</p>
<p>So I decided years ago that I wanted to react to &ldquo;CSS is hard&rdquo; by getting
better at CSS and taking it seriously as a technology, instead of devaluing it.
Doing that changed everything for me: I learned that so many of my frustrations
(&ldquo;centering is impossible&rdquo;) had been addressed in CSS a long time ago, and that
also what &ldquo;centering&rdquo; means is not always straightforward and it makes sense
that there are many ways to do it. CSS is hard because it&rsquo;s solving a hard
problem!</p>
<p>I&rsquo;ve been so impressed by the new CSS features that have been built in the last
10-15 years (some of which I&rsquo;ve talked about in this post!) and how they make
it easier to use CSS, and spending the time to improve my CSS skills has been a
really cool experience.</p>
<p>And that post made me feel like Tailwind contributes to the devaluing of CSS
expertise, and like that&rsquo;s not something I want to be a part of, even if
Tailwind has been a useful tool for me personally. Especially in this time of
LLMs where it feels more important than ever to value humans&rsquo; expertise.</p>
<p>Another blog post criticizing Tailwind that influenced me:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://joshcollinsworth.com/blog/tailwind-is-smart-steering">Classic rock, Mario Kart, and why we can&rsquo;t agree on Tailwind</a></li>
</ul>
<h3 id="that-s-all-for-now">that&rsquo;s all for now!</h3>
<p>Thanks to <a href="https://melody.dev/">Melody Starling</a> who originally designed and wrote the CSS for
<a href="https://wizardzines.com">wizardzines.com</a>, everything cool and fun
about the site is thanks to Melody.</p>
<p>Also I read so many incredible blog posts about CSS while working on this (from <a href="https://css-tricks.com/">CSS Tricks</a>, <a href="https://www.smashingmagazine.com/">Smashing Magazine</a>, and more), I&rsquo;ve tried to link some of them
throughout this post and I really appreciate how much folks in the CSS community share their practices.</p>

---

### [Links to CSS colour palettes](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/05/04/css-colour-palettes/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>A while back I decided to stop using Tailwind for new projects and to just write
vanilla CSS instead.</p>
<p>But one thing I missed about Tailwind was the <a href="https://v2.tailwindcss.com/docs/customizing-colors#color-palette-reference">colour palette</a> (<a href="https://gist.github.com/jvns/9e59b2cd1fe12601084ba78dded072fe">here as CSS</a>).
If I wanted a light blue I could just use <code>blue-100</code> and if I didn&rsquo;t like it
maybe try <code>blue-200</code> or <code>blue-50</code>. I&rsquo;m not very good with colours so it makes
a big difference to me to have a reasonable colour palette that somebody who is
better at colour than me has thought about.</p>
<p>But I&rsquo;m also a little tired of those Tailwind colours, so I asked on Mastodon
today what other colour palettes were out there. And then a friend said they
wanted links to those colour palettes, so here&rsquo;s a blog post so my friend can
see them, and all the rest of you too :)</p>
<h3 id="my-favourites">my favourites</h3>
<p>The ones I liked the most were:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://uchu.style/">uchū</a> (<a href="https://code.webb.page/nevercease/uchu.git/tree/dist/uchu.css">css file</a>, <a href="https://code.webb.page/nevercease/uchu.git/about/documentation/FAQ.md">FAQ</a>)</li>
<li><a href="https://stephango.com/flexoki">flexoki</a> (<a href="https://github.com/kepano/flexoki/blob/main/css/flexoki.css">css file</a>)</li>
<li><a href="https://www.reasonable.work/artifacts/ra005-reasonable-colors/">reasonable colours</a>, which seems to have a focus on accessibility (<a href="https://github.com/matthewhowell/reasonable-colors/blob/master/reasonable-colors.css">css file</a>)</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="more-colour-palettes">more colour palettes</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://webawesome.com/docs/color-palettes">web awesome</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.radix-ui.com/colors/docs/palette-composition/scales">radix</a></li>
<li><a href="https://designsystem.digital.gov/design-tokens/color/system-tokens/">US web design systems</a></li>
<li><a href="https://m2.material.io/design/color/the-color-system.html">material design</a></li>
</ul>
<h3 id="colourscheme-generators">colourscheme generators</h3>
